\alpha

Figure 14
.
The tree
T^{\prime}
has
n_{T^{\prime}}=2
corollas, collapsing the green one yields
T_{1}
, collapsing the red one yields
T_{2}
, collapsing both yields
T_{3}
. Thus it will appear in
\widetilde{\delta}_{A_{0}}(\widetilde{\kappa}_{A_{0}}(\alpha))
four times, with total combinatorial coefficient
1+(-1)+(-1)+1=0
.

We claim that
c_{T^{\prime}}=0
for all trees with at least two leaves. Indeed, for a tree
T^{\prime}
let
n_{T^{\prime}}
denote the number of internal vertices connected to exactly two leaves. Note that
n_{T^{\prime}}=0
if and only if
T^{\prime}
is the tree with a single leaf at the root and no internal vertex. If
v
is such an internal vertex, then we call
v
together with the two adjacent leaves a corolla. Then
T^{\prime}
could be obtained from the tree
T
where we collapse the corolla of
v
into a leaf
\alpha
. Note that this operation changes the sign. In total, we will obtain the tree
T^{\prime}
exactly
2^{n_{T^{\prime}}}
times, but with different signs: If we collapse
k
corollas then there is a sign
(-1)^{k}
. Therefore the combinatorial coefficient of
T^{\prime}
is
c_{T^{\prime}}=\sum_{k\geq 0}^{n_{T^{\prime}}}(-1)^{k}{k\choose{n_{T^{\prime}}}}=0
. The other direction
\widetilde{\kappa}_{A_{0}}(\widetilde{\delta}_{A_{0}}(\alpha))=\alpha
is proven similarly.

As for point iii), we simply compute

d_{A_{0}}{\widetilde{\kappa}_{A_{0}}}(\alpha)=d_{A_{0}}\alpha+\frac{1}{2}d_{A_{0}}K_{A_{0}}[\alpha,\alpha]=d_{A_{0}}\alpha+\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]-K_{A_{0}}d_{A_{0}}[\alpha,\alpha]-p_{A_{0}}[\alpha,\alpha].

If
d_{A_{0}}\alpha=-\frac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]
, then the first two terms cancel and the latter two terms vanish because
[\alpha,\alpha]=-2d_{A_{0}}\alpha
is exact.
∎

D.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.4
: “horizontality” of Ray-Singer torsion

We first need some auxiliary results.

Lemma D.1
.

Given a path of flat connections
A_{t}
, one has the following formula for the infinitesimal change of the Ray-Singer torsion
\tau_{A_{t}}
:

(293)

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\det(\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{diag}}_{A_{0}\leftarrow A_{t}})\tau_{A_{t}}=\tau_{A_{0}}\,\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}(K_{A_{0}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}).

Here
\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{diag}}_{A_{0}\leftarrow A_{t}}\colon H_{A_{t}}\rightarrow H_{A_{0}}
is the projection to cohomology of the parallel transport of the connection
\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}
along the path
(A_{t-\tau},A_{t-\tau})
,
0\leq\tau\leq t
in
\mathrm{FC}^{\prime}\times\mathrm{FC}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition of Ray-Singer torsion,

(294)

\tau_{A_{t}}=\mu_{A_{t}}\prod_{p=0}^{3}\left({\det}^{\prime}_{\Omega^{p}}\Delta_{A_{t}}\right)^{\frac{-(-1)^{p}p}{2}}

with
\mu_{A_{t}}
the volume element in
\mathrm{Det}H^{\bullet}_{A_{t}}
corresponding to Hodge inner product. Note that in this lemma we are using the synchronized
(A_{t},A_{t})
Hodge decompositon. Since
\mathfrak{B}_{A_{t}\leftarrow A_{0}}^{\mathrm{diag}}
is an isometry (Proposition
2.32
(
b
)), we have
\det(\mathfrak{B}_{A_{t}\leftarrow A_{0}})\mu_{A_{t}}=\mu_{A_{0}}
. Hence,

(295)

\tau_{A_{0}}^{-1}\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\det(\mathfrak{B}_{A_{0}\leftarrow A_{t}})\tau_{A_{t}}=\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\log\prod_{p=0}^{3}\left({\det}^{\prime}_{\Omega^{p}}\Delta_{A_{t}}\right)^{\frac{-(-1)^{p}p}{2}}\\
=\sum_{p=0}^{3}\frac{-(-1)^{p}p}{2}\mathrm{tr}_{\Omega^{p}}(\dot{\Delta}G),

where

\dot{\Delta}\colon=\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\Delta_{A_{t}}=[\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}},d^{*}]_{+}+[d,\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\dot{A}_{0}}]_{+}.

Here we suppress the subscript
A_{0}
in
G,d,d^{*}
. Continuing the computation (
295
) we have

\cdots=\sum_{p=0}^{3}\frac{-(-1)^{p}p}{2}\Big(\operatorname{tr}_{\Omega^{p-1}}\underbrace{d^{*}G}_{K}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}+\operatorname{tr}_{\Omega^{p}}\underbrace{d^{*}G}_{K}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}+\operatorname{tr}_{\Omega^{p}}\underbrace{dG}_{K^{*}}\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\dot{A}_{0}}+\operatorname{tr}_{\Omega^{p+1}}\underbrace{dG}_{K^{*}}\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\dot{A}_{0}}\Big)\\
=\sum_{p=0}^{3}\underbrace{\frac{-(-1)^{p}p-(-1)^{p+1}(p+1)}{2}}_{\frac{(-1)^{p}}{2}}\operatorname{tr}_{\Omega^{p}}K\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}+\underbrace{\frac{-(-1)^{p}p-(-1)^{p-1}(p-1)}{2}}_{\frac{-(-1)^{p}}{2}}\operatorname{tr}_{\Omega^{p}}K^{*}\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\dot{A}_{0}}\\
=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}K\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}\underbrace{K^{*}\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\dot{A}_{0}}}_{*K\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}*}=\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}K\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}.

This proves (
293
).
∎

Remark D.2
.

Traces in the proof above should be understood as zeta-regularized traces. For instance,
\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}K\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}
should be understood as

(296)

\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}K\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}\colon=\lim_{s\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,u^{s}\,\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}d^{*}e^{-u\Delta_{A_{0}}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}.

However, by the results of Axelrod-Singer
[
AS91
]
, the singular terms of the heat kernel expansion are proportional to
\mathrm{id}\in\mathrm{End}(\mathfrak{g})
and hence vanish under the trace with
\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}
by unimodularity of
\mathfrak{g}
. Therefore, the zeta-regularized supertrace coincides with the point-splitting regularized supertrace that we use to define tadpoles in Feynman diagrams, cf. footnote
38
.

Lemma D.3
.

Given a path of flat connections
A_{t}
and
A^{\prime}
close
A_{0}
, we have

(297)

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\det(\mathfrak{B}_{A_{0}\leftarrow A_{t};A^{\prime}})\tau_{A_{t}}=\tau_{A_{0}}\,\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}(K_{A_{0},A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}).

Proof.

Consider a path
A^{\prime}_{s}
in
\mathrm{FC}^{\prime}
starting at
A^{\prime}_{0}=A_{0}
and ending at
A^{\prime}_{1}=A^{\prime}
(and staying close to
A_{0}
). Denote

f_{s}\colon=\tau_{A_{0}}^{-1}\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\det(\mathfrak{B}_{A_{0}\leftarrow A_{t};A^{\prime}_{s}})\tau_{A_{t}},\quad h_{s}\colon=\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}(K_{A_{0},A^{\prime}_{s}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}).

Note that Lemma
D.1
implies that
f_{0}=h_{0}
. To prove the result it suffices to show that
\frac{d}{ds}f_{s}=\frac{d}{ds}h_{s}
.
For the derivative of
h_{s}
we find

(298)

\frac{d}{ds}h_{s}=\mathrm{Str}(\frac{d}{ds}K_{A_{0},A^{\prime}_{s}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}})=\mathrm{Str}(\cancel{[d,K\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}G]}+P\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}G+G\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}P)\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}\\
=\mathrm{Str}\,P(\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}G\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}-\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}G\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}).

For
f_{s}
we have

(299)

f_{s}=\tau_{A_{0}}^{-1}\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\det(\mathrm{Hol}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}}(R_{s,t}))\cdot\det(\mathfrak{B}_{A_{0}\leftarrow A_{t};A_{0}})\tau_{A_{t}}\\
=f_{0}+\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\det\mathrm{Hol}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}}(R_{s,t}).

Here
R_{s,t}
is the (curved) rectangle in
\mathcal{U}
with sides (i)
(A_{\tau},A_{0})
with
0<\tau<t
, (ii)
(A_{t},A^{\prime}_{\sigma})
with
0<\sigma<s
, (iii)
(A_{t-\tau},A^{\prime}_{s})
with
0<\tau<t
, (iv)
(A_{0},A^{\prime}_{s-\sigma})
with
0<\sigma<s
;
\mathrm{Hol}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}}(R_{s,t})\in\mathrm{End}(\mathrm{Harm}_{A_{0},A_{0}})
stands for the holonomy of
\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}
around the rectangle.

Denote
\rho_{s,t,\epsilon}=R_{s+\epsilon,t}-R_{s,t}
(here difference is an operation on singular 1-chains) – a small rectangle with vertices at
(A_{0},A^{\prime}_{s})
,
(A_{t},A^{\prime}_{s})
,
(A_{t},A^{\prime}_{s+\epsilon})
,
(A_{0},A^{\prime}_{s+\epsilon})
.
Next, (
299
) implies

(300)

\frac{d}{ds}f_{s}=\left.\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\epsilon\,\partial t}\right|_{\epsilon=t=0}\det\mathrm{Hol}_{\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}}(\rho_{s,t,\epsilon})\\
=-\mathrm{Str}\,\iota_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}\iota_{\dot{A}_{0}}F_{\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}}\Big|_{(A_{0},A^{\prime}_{s})}=\mathrm{Str}\,P(\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}G\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}-\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{0}}G\mathrm{ad}^{*}_{\partial_{s}A^{\prime}_{s}}).

Here in the last step we used the result (
85
) for the curvature of
\nabla^{\mathrm{Harm}}
. Comparing with (
298
), we see that we have
\partial_{s}f_{s}=\partial_{s}h_{s}
which, together with the initial condition
f_{0}=h_{0}
implies the desired result
f_{1}=h_{1}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4
.

Let
A_{t}=\varphi(A,A^{\prime},t\alpha)
– a path of flat connections from
A
at
t=0
to
\widetilde{A}
at
t=1
. We want to show that

(301)

\det(\mathfrak{B}_{A\leftarrow A_{t};A^{\prime}})\circ\tau_{A_{t}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}\tau_{A}\exp\sum_{\gamma}\frac{2}{|\mathrm{Aut}(\gamma)|}\Phi_{\gamma,A,A^{\prime}}(t\alpha).

For
t=1
, this is the desired relation (
111
). Denote the l.h.s. of (
301
) by
\lambda_{t}
and the r.h.s. by
\mu_{t}
. We have
\lambda_{0}=\mu_{0}
, so it suffices to prove
\lambda_{t}^{-1}\partial_{t}\lambda_{t}=\mu_{t}^{-1}\partial_{t}\mu_{t}
.

We have

(302)

\frac{d}{dt}\lambda_{t}=\left.\frac{d}{d\epsilon}\right|_{\epsilon=0}\det\mathfrak{B}_{A\leftarrow A_{t};A^{\prime}}\circ(\det\mathfrak{B}_{A_{t}\leftarrow A_{t+\epsilon};A^{\prime}}\circ\tau_{A_{t}+\epsilon})\\
\underset{\mathrm{Lemma}\;\ref{lemma D.2}}{=}\det\mathfrak{B}_{A\leftarrow A_{t};A^{\prime}}\circ\tau_{A_{t}}\,\mathrm{Str}\,K_{A_{t},A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{t}}=\lambda_{t}\,\mathrm{Str}\,K_{A_{t},A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{t}}.

To analyze
\mu_{t}
, we first remark that

(303)

\exp\sum_{\gamma}\frac{2}{|\mathrm{Aut}(\gamma)|}\Phi_{\gamma,A,A^{\prime}}(t\alpha)=\mathrm{Sdet}_{\Omega^{\bullet}}(1+K_{A,A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{A_{t}-A}).

Indeed, log of the r.h.s. here is

\mathrm{Str}\,\log(1+K_{A,A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{A_{t}-A})=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{-1}{n}\mathrm{Str}(-K_{A,A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{A_{t}-A})^{n}

– twice the sum of one-loop graphs, with
n\geq 1
trees plugged into the cycle.

From (
303
) we find

(304)

\frac{d}{dt}\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}\mathrm{Str}\underbrace{(1+K_{A,A^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{A_{t}-A})^{-1}K_{A,A^{\prime}}}_{K_{A_{t},A^{\prime}}}\mathrm{ad}_{\dot{A}_{t}}.

Comparing with (
302
), we see that
\lambda_{t}^{-1}\dot{\lambda}_{t}=\mu_{t}^{-1}\dot{\mu}_{t}
.
55
55

55

The r.h.s. here is regularized via point-splitting and the l.h.s. is defined via zeta-regularization. By Remark
D.2
, they coincide.

This finishes the proof.
∎
